This ideal  $I_{B|A}$  commutes with localizations by 3.13(c), and therefore, given a morphism  $X \xrightarrow{\ f\ } Y$  , we obtain the ideal sheaf  $\underline{I}_{X|Y}$  . If  $f$  is a morphism of essentially finite type, then  $f$  is smooth at  $x$  if and only if  $x \notin \mathrm{Supp}\ \underline{O}_X/\underline{O}_{X|Y}$  by 3.13(a). This notion is in an obvious way carried over to adic formal schemes of essentially
finite type and thus it is equally suitable for deformation theory.


6.  \underline{Non-degenerate quadratic singularities}

The objective in this section is to investigate the deformations of a $k$-scheme having non-degenerate quadratic singularities only. In view of 4.13, it becomes purely a local problem in an appropriate situation.

We go back to the notations of  §4. Thus  $\Lambda$  is a fixed complete noetherian local ring with the residue field  $k$  , and  $\underline{C}_\Lambda$  stands for the category of local artinian $\Lambda$-algebras with the same residue field  $k$  .

Let  $X$  be a $k$-scheme of finite type, and let  $x$  be a closed point of $X$  . We recall that the point  $x$  is said to be a non-degenerate quadratic singu-larity rational over  $k$  if  $\hat{O}_{X,x}$ $-$ $k$ $[[x_1,\ldots,x_n]]/(f)$  with $(\partial f/\partial x_1,\ldots,\partial f/\partial x_n) = (x_1,\ldots,x_n)$  . One  can always assume  $f$  to be a quadratic form. Indeed we have

\underline{Lemma} 6.1.  \underline{Let}  $f \in k[[x_1,\ldots,x_n]]$  . \underline{If}  $(\partial f/\partial x_1,\ldots,\partial f/\partial x_n) = M$  $(= (x_1,\ldots,x_n))$, \underline{then one can find}  $x_1',\ldots,x_n'$  \underline{with}  $x_i = x_i'$  (mod $M^2$)  \underline{such that} $f = q(x_1',\ldots,x_n')$  \underline{where}  $q$  \underline{is a quadratic form.}

\underline{Proof}:  We do this by a successive approximation. Write  $f = f_2 + h_3$  where  $f_2$ is a quadratic form and  $h_3 \in M^3$  . Since  $(\partial f/\partial x_1,\ldots,\partial f/\partial x_n) = M$  , there exists $\epsilon_1^{(1)},\ldots,\epsilon_n^{(1)}$  in  $M^2$  such that  $\Sigma\ \epsilon_i^{(1)}\ \partial f/\partial x_i \equiv h_3$  (mod $M^4$)  . If we set $X_i^{(1)} = X_i - \epsilon_i^{(1)}$  , then  $f(x_1^{(1)},\ldots,x_n^{(1)}) =$ $f(x_1,\ldots,x_n) - \Sigma\ \epsilon_i^{(1)}\ \partial f/\partial x_i + \ldots \equiv f_2(x_1,\ldots,x_n)$  (mod $M^4$)  i.e., $f(x_1^{(1)},\ldots,x_n^{(1)}) \equiv f_2(x_1,\ldots,x_n) + h_4$  with  $h_4 \in M^4$  . Now $(\partial f/\partial x_1^{(1)},\ldots,\partial f/\partial x_n^{(1)}) = (\partial f/\partial x_1,\ldots,\partial f/\partial x_n) = M$  and therefore we can choose
$\varepsilon_1^{(2)},\dots,\varepsilon_n^{(2)}$  in  $M^3$  such that  $\Sigma\ \varepsilon_i^{(2)}\ \partial f/\partial x_u^{(1)} \equiv h_4\ (\mathrm{mod}\ M^5)$ . Then $f(x_1^{(1)} - \varepsilon_1^{(2)},\dots,x_n^{(1)} - \varepsilon_n^{(2)}) = f(x_1^{(1)},\dots,x_n^{(1)}) - \Sigma\ \varepsilon_i^{(2)}\partial f/\partial x_i^{(1)} + \dots \equiv f_2(x_1,\dots,x_n)\ (\mathrm{mod}\ M^5)$ . Continuing this process we can find $\varepsilon_i^{(1)},\ \varepsilon_i^{(2)},\ \varepsilon_i^{(3)},\dots$  $(i = 1,2,\dots,n)$  with  $\varepsilon_i^{(v)} \in M^{v+1}$  such that if we set $x_i^{(v)} = x_i - (\varepsilon_i^{(1)} + \dots + \varepsilon_i^{(v)})$  then  $f(x_1^{(v)},\dots,x_n^{(v)}) \equiv f_2(x_1,\dots,x_n)$ $(\mathrm{mod}\ M^{v+3})$ . Thus if we set  $\varepsilon_i = \sum_{v} \varepsilon_i^{(v)}$  then  $f(x_1-\varepsilon_1,\dots,x_n-\varepsilon_n) =$ $= f_2(x_1,\dots,x_n)$  i.e.,  $f(x_1,\dots,x_n) = f_2(x_1+\varepsilon_1,\dots,x_n+\varepsilon_n)$ .

\underline{Lemma} 6.2.   \underline{Let}  $A_0$  \underline{be a local k-algebra, essentially of finite type such that}

\begin{equation*}
\hat{A}_0 \simeq k[[x_1,\dots,x_n]]/(f)
\end{equation*}
\underline{where}  $f$  \underline{is a non-degenerate quadratic form, and let}  $(R,\ \mathfrak{X})$  \underline{be a formal versal deformation of}  $X_0 = \mathrm{Spec}(A_0)$  . \underline{Then}

(1)  $J_{\mathfrak{X}}(\mathfrak{X}|R) \rightarrow J_R(\mathfrak{X}|R) \rightarrow \mathrm{Spec}(\Lambda)$  \underline{are both isomorphisms.}

(2)  $J_R(\mathfrak{X}|R)$  \underline{is a principal ideal in}  $R$  \underline{generated by a free formal generator of}  $R$  \underline{over}  $\Lambda$  i.e.  $I_R(\mathfrak{X}|R) = (t)$  \underline{and}  $R = \Lambda[[t]]$  .

(3)  $\mathfrak{X} \rightarrow \mathrm{Spec}(\Lambda)$  \underline{is formally smooth.}

\underline{Proof:}  We set  $\mathfrak{X} = \mathrm{Spf}(A)$  . By 4.10(b),  $(R,\ \mathrm{Spf}(\hat{A}))$  is a formal versal deformation of  $\hat{X}_0 = \mathrm{Spec}(\hat{A}_0)$  where  $\hat{A}_0 = \varprojlim_{v} A \otimes_R R_v$  . Now  $\hat{A}_0 \simeq k[[x_1,\dots,x_n]]/(f)$  and  $(\partial f/\partial x_1,\dots,\partial f/\partial x_n) = (x_1,\dots,x_n)$  in $k[[x_1,\dots,x_n]]$  . It follows readily that  $[\underline{M}_{\hat{X}_0}(k[\varepsilon])] \simeq k$  and, by 4.14, we get

\begin{equation*}
R \simeq \Lambda[[t]] \quad\text{and}\quad \hat{A} \simeq R[[x_1,\dots,x_n]]/(F - t)
\end{equation*}
where  $F$  is a quadratic polynomial in  $\Lambda[x_1,\dots,x_n]$  $(\subset R[[x_1,\dots,x_n]])$ obtained from  $f$  by lifting the coefficients to  $\Lambda$  . Now

\begin{equation*}
0 \rightarrow \hat{A} \ \overset{df}{\longrightarrow}\ \hat{A}dx_1 \oplus \dots \oplus \hat{A}dx_n \ \xrightarrow{\hspace{3em}}\ \Omega_{\hat{A}|R} \rightarrow 0 
\end{equation*}


 is exact where  $df : \hat{A} \rightarrow \hat{A}dx_1 \oplus \dots \oplus \hat{A}dx_n$  is given by $1 \rightarrow (\partial f/\partial x_1)dx_1 + \dots + (\partial f/\partial x_n)dx_n$  , and therefore $I_{\hat{A}}(\hat{A}|R) = (\partial F/\partial x_1,\dots,\partial F/\partial x_n) = (x_1,\dots,x_n)$  , and hence $\hat{A}/I_{\hat{A}}(\hat{A}|R) = \hat{A}/(x_1,\dots,x_n) = R[[x_1,\dots,x_n]]/(x_1,\dots,x_n,F - t) \simeq \Lambda$ i.e., the canonical map  $\Lambda \rightarrow \hat{A}/I_{\hat{A}}(\hat{A}|R)$  is an isomorphism. It follows that $\hat{A} \rightarrow A/I_A(A|R)$  is an isomorphism, and consequently  $\Lambda \rightarrow R/I_R(A|R)$  is also an isomorphism. This proves (1) and (2). On the other hand, (3) follows from $\hat{A} \simeq R[[x_1,\dots,x_n]]/(F - t) \simeq \Lambda[[t,x_1,\dots,x_n]]/(F - t) \simeq \Lambda[[x_1,\dots,x_n]]$   as $\Lambda$-algebras.

\underline{Corollary} 6.3.  \underline{Let}  $X_0$  \underline{be a k-scheme of finite type, and let}  $x$  \underline{in}  $X_0$  \underline{be a non-degenerate quadratic singularity rational over}  $k$  .

(1)  \underline{Let}  $(R,\ \mathfrak{X})$ ,  $(R,\ \mathfrak{Y})$  \underline{be any two deformations of}  $X_0$  \underline{where} $R \in \mathrm{ob}(\hat{\underline{C}}_{\Lambda})$  . \underline{Assume that}  $k = k^2$  . \underline{Then}  $\mathfrak{X}_{(\mathfrak{x})} \simeq \mathfrak{Y}_{(\mathfrak{x})}$  \underline{as R-adic formal schemes if and only if}  $I_R(\mathfrak{X}_{(\mathrm{x})}|R) = I_R(\mathfrak{Y}_{(\mathrm{x})}|R)$  .

(2)  \underline{Let}  $R \in \mathrm{ob}(\hat{\underline{C}}_{\Lambda})$  \underline{be a regular local ring, and let}  $(R,\ \mathfrak{X})$  \underline{be a deformation of}  $X_0$  . \underline{Then}  $O_{\mathfrak{X},x}$  \underline{is a regular local ring if and only if} $I_R(\mathfrak{X}_{(\mathrm{x})}|R) \not\subset \underline{m}_R^{\,2}$  i.e., \underline{if and only if}  $I_R(\mathfrak{X}_{(\mathrm{x})}|R)$  \underline{is a regular divisor in} $\mathrm{Spf}(R)$  .

\underline{Proof:}  Let  $(R_0,\ Z)$  be a formal versal deformation of  $\mathrm{Spec}(\hat{\underline{O}}_{X_0,x})$  . Since  $x$ in  $X_0$  is a non-degenerate quadratic singularity rational over  $k$  , we may assume that  $R_0 \simeq \Lambda[[t]]$  and  $Z \simeq \mathrm{Spf}(R_0[[x_1,\dots,x_n]]/(F - t))$  as in 6.2.

(1)  $(R,\ \mathfrak{X}_{(\mathfrak{x})})$ ,  $(R,\ \mathfrak{Y}_{(\mathfrak{x})})$  are deformations of  $\mathrm{Spec}(\hat{\underline{O}}_{X_0,x})$  , and therefore they are induced from  $(R_0,\ Z)$  by continuous local $\Lambda$-algebra maps $\varphi,\ \psi : R_0 \rightarrow R$  respectively, i.e., if we set  $a = \varphi(t)$  ,  $b = \psi(t)$  , then

\begin{equation*}
\mathfrak{X}_{(\mathfrak{x})} \simeq \mathrm{Spf}(R[[X_1,\dots,X_n]]/(R - a)) \quad ,
\end{equation*}
\begin{equation*}
\mathfrak{Y}_{(\mathfrak{x})} \simeq \mathrm{Spf}(R[[X_1,\dots,X_n]]/(F - b))
\end{equation*}
as $R$-adic formal schemes. Assume that  $I_R(\mathfrak{X}_{(\mathrm{x})}|R) = I_R(\mathfrak{Y}_{(\mathrm{x})}|R)$  i.e.,
$(a) = (b)$ in $R$ . Then $a = ab$ for some unit $u$ in $R$ . Since $k = k^2$ and $R$ is complete, we can write $u = v^2$ for some unit $v$ in $R$ , i.e., $a = v^2b$ . Then $(F - b) = (v^2F - a)$ , so that $X_i \to vX_i$ establishes an $R$-algebra isomorphism $R[[X_1,\ldots,X_n]]/(F - a) \;\overset{\sim}{\relbar\relbar\relbar}\; R[[X_1,\ldots,X_n]]/(F - b)$ since $F$ is a quadratic form. The other direction is trivial.

(2)  There exists a continuous local $\Lambda$-algebra map $\varphi : R_0 = \Lambda[[t]] \to R$ such that $\mathfrak{X}_{(\mathfrak{x})} \simeq \mathrm{Spf}(R[[X_1,\ldots,X_n]]/(F - \varphi(t)))$ . We note that $I_R(\mathfrak{X}_{(x)}|R) = I_R(\mathfrak{X}_{(\mathfrak{x})}|R) = (\varphi(t))$ , and that $R[[X_1,\ldots,X_n]]/(F - \varphi(t)) = \hat{\underline{O}}_{\mathfrak{X},x}$ is regular if and only if $\underline{O}_{\mathfrak{X},x}$ is regular. Now $R$ is regular and hence $R[[X_1,\ldots,X_n]]$ is also a regular local ring. Thus if we set $M$ to be the maximal ideal of $R[[X_1,\ldots,X_n]]$ , then $R[[X_1,\ldots,X_n]]/(F - \varphi(t))$ is regular if and only if $F - \varphi(t)$ is not in $M^2$ , i.e. if and only if $\varphi(t) \notin \underline{m}_R^{\,2}$ , i.e., if and only if $I_R(\mathfrak{X}_{(x)}|R) \notin \underline{m}_R^{\,2}$ .

\underline{Theorem} 6.4.  \underline{Let} $X_0$ \underline{be a k-scheme of finite type with isolated non-smooth points only, and let} $(R, \mathfrak{X})$ \underline{be a formal versal deformation of} $X_0$ . \underline{Denote by} $\{z_1,\ldots,z_m\}$ \underline{the set of non-smooth points in} $X_0$ . \underline{Assume that} $H^2(X_0, \underline{D}^0_{X_0}) = 0$ , \underline{and all} $z_i$'s \underline{are non-degenerate quadratic singularity rational over} $k$ . \underline{Then}

(1)  $R$ \underline{is formally smooth over} $\Lambda$ i.e., $R$ \underline{is a formal power-series ring over} $\Lambda$ ;

(2)  $J_{\mathfrak{X}}(\mathfrak{X}|R) = \coprod_{1\leq i\leq m} J_{\mathfrak{X}_{(z_i)}}(\mathfrak{X}_{(z_i)}|R)$  \underline{(disjoint union), and}

$J_{\mathfrak{X}_{(z_i)}}(\mathfrak{X}_{(z_i)}|R) \;\overset{\sim}{\relbar\relbar\relbar}\; J_R(\mathfrak{X}_{(z_i)}|R)$  \underline{is an isomorphism};

(3)  $I_R(\mathfrak{X}_{(z_i)}|R)$ \underline{is a principal ideal and in turn so is}

$I_R(\mathfrak{X}|R) = \prod_{1\leq\ i\leq\ m} I_R(\mathfrak{X}_{(z_i)}|R)$ . \underline{Furthermore if we set} $I_R(\mathfrak{X}_{(z_i)}|R) = (t_i)$ , \underline{then} $t_1,\ldots,t_m$ \underline{can be extended to a system of free formal generators of} $R$ \underline{over} $\Lambda$ \underline{(as formal power-series ring over} $\Lambda$ ).

(4)  $\mathfrak{X} \to \mathrm{Spec}(\Lambda)$ \underline{is smooth}, i.e. $J_{\mathfrak{X}}(\mathfrak{X}|\Lambda) = \varphi$ .



(5)  \underline{If} $X_0$ \underline{is a complete irreducible curve, then} $\dim \underline{M}_{X_0} = 3g - 3 + a$ \underline{where} $g = \dim_k H^1(X_0, \underline{O}_{X_0})$ \underline{and} $a = \dim_k H^0(X_0, \underline{D}^0_{X_0})$ .

\underline{Proof:}  (1) follows from (2) and (3): The first statement of (2) follows from 5.7(2). Now let $(R_i, \mathfrak{Y}_i)$ be a formal versal deformation of $\mathrm{Spec}(\hat{\underline{O}}_{X_0,z_i})$ . Since $(R, \mathfrak{X}_{(\mathfrak{z}_i)})$ is a deformation of $\mathrm{Spec}(\hat{\underline{O}}_{X_0,z_i})$ , there exists a continuous local $\Lambda$-algebra maps $\varphi_i = R_i \to R$ with a cartesian diagram

\begin{center}
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=huge, row sep=huge]
\mathfrak{X}_{(\mathfrak{z}_i)} \arrow[r] \arrow[d] & \mathfrak{Y}_i \arrow[d] \\
\mathrm{Spf}(R) \arrow[r, "{\mathrm{Spf}(\varphi_i)}"'] & \mathrm{Spf}(R_i)
\end{tikzcd}
\end{center}
Therefore we have

\begin{equation*}
J_{\mathfrak{X}_{(z_i)}}(\mathfrak{X}_{(z_i)}|R) = J_{\mathfrak{X}_{(\mathfrak{z}_i)}}(\mathfrak{X}_{(\mathfrak{z}_i)}|R) \simeq J_{\mathfrak{Y}_i}(\mathfrak{Y}_i|R_i) \underset{\mathrm{Spf}(R_i)}{\times} \mathrm{Spf}(R)
\end{equation*}
\begin{equation*}
J_R(\mathfrak{X}_{(z_i)}|R) = J_R(\mathfrak{X}_{(\mathfrak{z}_i)}|R) \simeq J_{R_i}(\mathfrak{Y}_i|R_i) \underset{\mathrm{Spr}(R_i)}{\times} \mathrm{Spf}(R) \quad .
\end{equation*}
Since $J_{\mathfrak{Y}_i}(\mathfrak{Y}_i|R_i) \to J_{R_i}(\mathfrak{Y}_i|R_i)$ is an isomorphism by 6.2(1), we have that the canonical morphism $J_{\mathfrak{X}_{(z_i)}}(\mathfrak{X}_{(z_i)}|R) \to J_R(\mathfrak{X}_{(z_i)}|R)$ is an isomorphism. Now $I_{R_i}(\mathfrak{Y}_i|R_i) = (u_i) = (u_i)$ with $R_i = \Lambda[[u_i]]$ by 6.2(2), and therefore

\begin{equation*}
I_R(\mathfrak{X}_{(z_i)}|R) = I_R(\mathfrak{X}_{(\mathfrak{z}_i)}|R) = (t_i)
\end{equation*}
where $t_i = \varphi_i(u_i)$ , and therefore $I_R(\mathfrak{X}|R) = \prod_{1\leq\ i\leq\ m} I_R(\mathfrak{X}_{(z_i)}|R) = (\ \prod_{i=1}^{n} t_i\ )$ .

Now $\underline{M}_{X_0} \to \underline{M}_{X_0,(\mathfrak{z}_1)} \times \ldots \times \underline{M}_{X_0,(\mathfrak{z}_m)}$ is smooth by and therefore $R_1 \hat{\otimes}_{\Lambda} \ldots \hat{\otimes}_{\Lambda} R_m \to R$ is formally smooth by 4.13, which implies that $t_1,\ldots,t_m$ is extended to a system of free formal generators of $R$ over $\Lambda$ .



(4)  The projection morphism  $\mathcal{X}_{(\mathcal{Z}_i)} \overset{\sim}{\rightarrow} \mathcal{Y}_1 \times_{\mathrm{Spf}(R_1)} \mathrm{Spf}(R) \rightarrow \mathcal{Y}_i$  is formally smooth since  $\mathrm{Spf}(R) \xrightarrow{\ \mathrm{Spf}(\varphi_i)\ } \mathrm{Spf}(R_i)$  is formally smooth. On the other hand, $\mathcal{Y}_i \rightarrow \mathrm{Spec}(\Lambda)$  is formally smooth by 6.2(3)  and therefore  $\mathcal{X}_{(\mathcal{Z}_i)} \rightarrow \mathrm{Spec}(\Lambda)$  is formally smooth. Consequently  $\mathcal{X} \rightarrow \mathrm{Spec}(\Lambda)$  is formally smooth since  $\{z_1,\dots,z_m\}$  is the set of non-smooth points of  $X_0$ .

(5)  Since  $\overline{R}$  is formally smooth over  $\Lambda$  and

\begin{equation*}
0 \rightarrow H^1(X_0,\ \underline{D}^0_{X_0})\ \rightarrow [\underline{M}_{X_0}(k[\varepsilon])] \rightarrow H^0(X_0,\ \underline{D}^1_{X_0}) \rightarrow 0
\end{equation*}
is exact by 4.4 and 4.11, we have

\begin{equation*}
\begin{aligned}
\dim \underline{M}_{X_0} \ &= \ \dim k \otimes R \ = \ \dim_k H^1(X_0,\ \underline{D}^0_{X_0}) + \dim H^0(X_0,\ \underline{D}^1_{X_0})\\
&= \ \dim_k H^1(X_0,\ \underline{D}^0_{X_0}) + m \ .
\end{aligned}
\end{equation*}
Then it suffices to show that

\begin{equation*}
\dim_k H^1(X_0,\ \underline{D}^0_{X_0}) + m = 3g - 3 + \dim_k H^0(X_0,\ \underline{D}^0_{X_0})
\end{equation*}
i.e.,  $\chi(\underline{D}^0_{X_0}) = 3 - 3g + m$

where we set  $\chi(\underline{E}) = \dim_k H^0(X_0,\ \underline{E}) - \dim_k H^1(X_0,\ \underline{E})$  for any coherent  $X_0$-module  $\underline{E}$ . Since  $\lambda(\underline{D}^0_{X_0})$  does not change when one replaces the base field  $k$  by its algebraic closure, we may assume that  $k$  is algebraically closed. Set  $A = \underline{O}_{X_0,z}$ , where  $z$  is a non-smooth point of  $X_0$ , and let  $A'$  be its normalization. By hypothesis, we have  $A \simeq k[[x,y]]/(f)$  where  $f$  is a non-degenerate quadratic form. Since  $k$  is algebraically closed, we may assume that  $f = xy$ , i.e., $A \simeq k[[x,y]]/(xy)$ . From this we conclude immediately that  $\ell(A'/A) = 1$  and  $\mathrm{Der}_k(A,\ \underline{m}_A) = \mathrm{Der}_k(A,\ A)$ . Let  $C$  be the conductor of  $A$  in  $A'$ . Since $A$ is a complete-intersection we must have that  $\ell(A/C) = \ell(A'/A) = 1$  so that  $C = \underline{m}_A$ . We thus obtain that  $A' = \{a' \in K |\ a'\underline{m}_A \subset \underline{m}_A\}$  where  $K$  is the fraction field of  $A$ . We now claim that  $\mathrm{Der}_k(A,\ A) \subset \mathrm{Der}_k(A',\ A')$  as $A$-modules contained in  $\mathrm{Der}_k(K,\ K)$ . Indeed, let  $X \in \mathrm{Der}_k(A,\ A)$ . Then for any  $a' \in A'$  and
$x \in \underline{m}_A$  we have  $xa' \in \underline{m}_A$  so that  $X(xa') \in \underline{m}_A$  and  $a'X(x) \in \underline{m}_A$  since  $\mathrm{Der}_k(A,\ \underline{m}_A) = \mathrm{Der}_k(A,\ A)$ . Consequently  $X(xa') = a'X(x) + xX(a')$  entails that  $xX(a') \in \underline{m}_A$  for all  $x \in \underline{m}_A$ , and therefore  $X(a') \in A'$ . Furthermore, the exact sequence

\begin{equation*}
0 \ \rightarrow\ \mathrm{Der}_k(A,\ A) \rightarrow \mathrm{Der}_k(A',\ A') \rightarrow A'/\underline{m}_A A' \rightarrow\ 0
\end{equation*}
entails the exact sequence

\begin{equation*}
0 \ \rightarrow\ \mathrm{Der}_k(A,\ A) \rightarrow \mathrm{Der}_k(A',\ A') \rightarrow A'/C \rightarrow 0
\end{equation*}
Therefore if we set  $X'_0$  to be the normalization of  $X_0$ , then

\begin{equation*}
0 \rightarrow \underline{D}^0_{X_0} \rightarrow \underline{D}^0_{X'_0}\ \rightarrow \underline{O}_{X'_0}/C \rightarrow 0
\end{equation*}
is exact. Since  $X_0$  is locally a complete-intersection, we have  $\ell(\underline{O}_{X'_0}/C) = 2\ell(\underline{O}_{X'_0}/\underline{O}_{X_0}) = 2m$ , whereas  $\chi(\underline{D}^0_{X'_0}) = 3 - 3(g-m)$  by Riemann-Roch theorem. Consequently

\begin{equation*}
\chi(\underline{D}^0_{X_0}) = \chi(\underline{D}^0_{X'_0}) - \ell(\underline{O}_{X'_0}/C) = 3 - 3(g-m) - 2m = 3 - 3g + m \ .
\end{equation*}
This completes our proof.

\underline{Remark} 6.5.  In the formula 6.4(5), the number  $a = H^0(X_0,\ \underline{D}^0_{X_0})$  can be easily computed in terms of Riemann-Roch theorem. Thus

\begin{equation*}
a = \left\{ \begin{array}{lll} 0 & \text{if} & g \geq 2 \\[4pt] 1 & \text{if} & g = 1 \\[4pt] 3 & \text{if} & g = 0 \end{array} \right.
\end{equation*}
Finally we add a remark on characteristic 2. If the characteristic of the base field  $k$  is  $2$ , there is no non-degenerate quadratic form on  $n$  variables if  $n$  is odd. In other words there does not exist a non-degenerate quadratic singularity of even dimension if  $char k = 2$ . In this case one may consider a quadratic form of maximum non-degeneracy: 



\underline{Definition} 6.6.  \underline{Let}  $X$  \underline{be a k-scheme of finite type.} \underline{A closed point}  $z \in X$  \underline{is called an ordinary quadratic singularity rational over}  $k$  \underline{if the following holds:}

(i)  \underline{If either}  $char k \neq 2$  \underline{or}  $char k = 2$  \underline{and}  $dim O_{X,z}$  \underline{is odd, then} $a$  \underline{is a non-degenerate quadratic singularity rational over}  $k$  .

(ii)  \underline{If}  $char k = 2$  \underline{and}  $dim O_{X,z}$  \underline{is even, then} $\bar{k} \underset{k}{\otimes} \hat{O}_{X,z} \simeq \bar{k}[[X_0, X_1, \ldots, X_{2n}]]/(f)$  \underline{with}

\begin{equation*}
f = X_0^2 + X_1X_2 + X_3X_4 + \ldots + X_{2n-1}X_{2n}
\end{equation*}
\underline{where}  $\bar{k}$  \underline{is the algebraic closure of}  $k$  .

\underline{Lemma} 6.7.  \underline{Let}  $k$  \underline{be a field of characteristic}  $2$  \underline{and}  \underline{set} $A_0 = k[[X_0, X_1, \ldots ,X_{2n}]]/(f)$  \underline{where}

\begin{equation*}
f = X_0^2 + X_1X_2 + X_3X_4 + \ldots + X_{2n-1}X_{2n} \quad ,
\end{equation*}
\underline{and let}  $(R, \mathfrak{X})$  \underline{be a formal versal deformation of}  $X_0 = \mathrm{Spec}(A_0)$  . \underline{Then} $R = \Lambda[[t, t_1]]$  , \underline{where}  $t, t_1$  \underline{are free formal generators of}  $R$  \underline{over}  $\Lambda$  , \underline{such} \underline{that}  $I_R(\mathfrak{X}|R) = (t^2)$  , \underline{and}  $\mathfrak{X} \to \mathrm{Spec}(\Lambda)$  \underline{is formally smooth.}

\underline{Proof:}  We set  $\mathfrak{X} = \mathrm{Spf}(A)$  . Since

\begin{equation*}
(f, \frac{\partial f}{\partial X_0} , \ldots , \frac{\partial f}{\partial X_{2n}} ) = (X_0^2, X_1, \ldots , X_{2n}) \quad ,
\end{equation*}
it follows that  $D^1(A_0|k, A_0) \simeq  k[X_0]/(X_0^2)$  and therefore  $[\underline{M}_{X_0}(k[\epsilon])]$  is 2-dimensional vector space over  $k$  . Furthermore, it follows from 4.14 that

\begin{equation*}
R \simeq \Lambda[[t, t_1]] \quad \text{and} \quad A \simeq R[[X_0, X_1, \ldots ,X_{2n}]]/(F)
\end{equation*}
where  $F = f + t_1 + tX_0$  . Now the exact sequence

\begin{center}
\begin{tikzcd}[column sep=large]
0 \arrow[r] & A \arrow[r, "dF"] & AdX_0 \oplus \ldots \oplus AdX_{2n} \arrow[r] & \hat{\Omega}_{A|R} \arrow[r] & 0
\end{tikzcd}
\end{center}
where

\begin{equation*}
dF = 1 \to \sum_{0 \leq i \leq 2n} \frac{\partial f}{\partial X_i} dX_i + dX_0 \quad , 
\end{equation*}


 entails that

\begin{equation*}
I_A(A|R) = (t, x_1, x_2, \ldots, x_{2n}) = (x_0^2 + t_1, t, x_1, \ldots, x_{2n})
\end{equation*}
so that

\begin{equation*}
A/I_A(A|R) \simeq A/(X_0^2 + t_1, t, x_1, \ldots, x_{2n}) \simeq \Lambda[[t_1, X_0]]/(X_0^2 - t_1)  \ .
\end{equation*}
Since  $\Lambda[[t_1, X_0]]/(X_0^2 - t_1) \simeq R/(t) \oplus R/(t)$  as R-modules, it follows that $I_R(A|R) = (t^2)$  , which proves our first assertion. On the other hand,

\begin{equation*}
\begin{aligned} A &\simeq \Lambda[[t, t_1, x_o, x_1, \ldots, x_{2n}]]/(X_0^2 + X_1X_2 + \ldots + X_{2n-1}X_{2n} + tX_0 + t_1) \\ &\simeq \Lambda[[t, X_0, X_1,\ldots,X_{2n}]] \end{aligned}
\end{equation*}
and therefore  $A$  is formally smooth over  $\Lambda$  .

The above statement 6.7 yields a similar result as 6.4, proof of which we leave to the readers.

\underline{Corollary} 6.8.  \underline{Let}  $X_0$  \underline{be a k-scheme of finite type with isolated non-smooth points only such that}  $H^2(X_0, \underline{D}^0_{X_0}) = 0$  , \underline{and let}  $(R, \mathfrak{X})$  \underline{be a formal versal deformation of}  $X_0$  . \underline{Denote by}  $\{z_1, \ldots, z_m\}$  \underline{the set of non-smooth points in} $X_0$  , \underline{and assume that}  $char k = 2$  , $dim X_0$  \underline{is even, and all}  $z_i$'s  \underline{are ordinary quadratic singularities. Then}

(1)  $R$  \underline{is formally smooth over}  $\Lambda$

(2)  $I_R(\mathfrak{X}_{(z_i)}|R)$  \underline{is a principal ideal and in turn so is}

$I_R(\mathfrak{X}|R) = \mathop{\hphantom{\bigcap}}_{1 \leq i \leq m} I_R(\mathfrak{X}_{(z_i)}|R)$  . \underline{Furthermore,}  $I_R(\mathfrak{X}_{(z_i)}|R) = (t_i^2)$  \underline{and} $t_1, \ldots , t_m$  \underline{can be extended to a system of free formal generators of}  $R$  \underline{over} $\Lambda$  .

(3)  $\mathfrak{X} \to \mathrm{Spec}(\Lambda)$  \underline{is formally smooth}

(4)  \underline{If}  $k$  \underline{is algebraically closed and}  $X_0$  \underline{is a complete irreducible curve, then}  $dim \underline{M}_{X_0} = 3g - 3 + a$  \underline{where}  $g = dim_k H^1(X_0, \underline{O}_{X_0})$  \underline{and} $a = dim_k H^0(X_0, \underline{D}^0_{X_0})$  .
